People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. My supervisor showed me the camera and said nothing was there, nothing was happening, and I tried to accept that… I really did.\n\nBut even after he showed me, I still felt sure about it. Like I couldn’t shake it. Does that make sense the way I’m saying it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear, even when you’re sure there’s nothing actually making those sounds?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. Not all the time, but… sometimes I hear a man’s voice, like it’s coming from the vents or maybe the mop closet at work.\n\nIt’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything. And I know that probably sounds bad, but it feels real when it happens—like someone is standing right next to me saying it. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… that’s just how it sounds to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your mood changing a lot, like feeling very down or very excited, along with these experiences where you hear voices or feel watched?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they kind of came together sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but there were times I was only sleeping a few hours and I had all these big plans for the buildings, like I was going to fix things or organize everything better.\n\nAnd around then, that’s when I’d hear the voice in the vents too… and I felt real sure the hallway lights were sending warnings about me. I know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so down or hopeless that you lost interest in things you usually care about, or felt that life wasn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, sometimes. I don’t like saying it because I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be a problem or scare anybody.\n\nBut when it gets really dark for me, I’ll catch myself thinking maybe it’d be better if I just didn’t wake up. Or like… if I could just disappear for good, maybe that would be easier. I’m not proud of thinking that. I’m just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood—like feeling really down or really up—happened even when you weren’t having those experiences with the voices or feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I think most of the time when my mood gets really off, that other stuff is around too, so it’s hard for me to separate it.\n\nThere might’ve been days I was just down or kind of keyed up without noticing anything else, but I don’t know if I can say for sure. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was really low or really high, but you weren’t hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I can’t really think of a clean example like that. I don’t want to say no if I’m just not remembering right, but… when my mood gets that far one way or the other, it’s usually all mixed together in my head.\n\nMaybe there were little times, like a bad day or feeling kind of wound up, but not the real strong ones. I don’t know if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was very low or very high, but you were not experiencing any of the other things like hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can give you that exact kind of example, doctor. Does that make sense the way I’m saying it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear, even when you’re sure there’s nothing actually making those sounds?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. Not all the time, but… sometimes I hear a man’s voice, like it’s coming from the vents or maybe the mop closet at work.\n\nIt’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything. And I know that probably sounds bad, but it feels real when it happens—like someone is standing right next to me saying it. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… that’s just how it sounds to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your mood changing a lot, like feeling very down or very excited, along with these experiences where you hear voices or feel watched?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they kind of came together sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but there were times I was only sleeping a few hours and I had all these big plans for the buildings, like I was going to fix things or organize everything better.\n\nAnd around then, that’s when I’d hear the voice in the vents too… and I felt real sure the hallway lights were sending warnings about me. I know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so down or hopeless that you lost interest in things you usually care about, or felt that life wasn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, sometimes. I’m just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood—like feeling really down or really up—happened even when you weren’t having those experiences with the voices or feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I think most of the time when my mood gets really off, that other stuff is around too, so it’s hard for me to separate it.\n\nThere might’ve been days I was just down or kind of keyed up without noticing anything else, but I don’t know if I can say for sure. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was really low or really high, but you weren’t hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I can’t really think of a clean example like that. I don’t want to say no if I’m just not remembering right, but… when my mood gets that far one way or the other, it’s usually all mixed together in my head.\n\nMaybe there were little times, like a bad day or feeling kind of wound up, but not the real strong ones. I don’t know if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was very low or very high, but you were not experiencing any of the other things like hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can give you that exact kind of example, doctor. But it gets all mixed up when I talk.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts ever feel disorganized or jumbled, like you can’t quite get them in order when you’re trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not all the time, but enough that people notice.\n\nLike I’ll try to say something simple about the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then a prayer rug, and then chess pieces… and I know that probably doesn’t sound right. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. I’m trying to make sense, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're certain about something that others say isn't true, like a belief or idea that you hold strongly, even if it doesn't make sense to the people around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m making trouble or anything. But there was this thing at the building.\n\nI felt pretty sure the building people had, like… marked me somehow. Like they were using the hallway lights, and little looks between them, to warn each other about me. My supervisor showed me the camera and said nothing was there, nothing was happening, and I tried to accept that… I really did.\n\nBut even after he showed me, I still felt sure about it. Like I couldn’t shake it. Does that make sense the way I’m saying it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear, even when you’re sure there’s nothing actually making those sounds?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, sometimes. Not all the time, but… sometimes I hear a man’s voice, like it’s coming from the vents or maybe the mop closet at work.\n\nIt’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything. And I know that probably sounds bad, but it feels real when it happens—like someone is standing right next to me saying it. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… that’s just how it sounds to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your mood changing a lot, like feeling very down or very excited, along with these experiences where you hear voices or feel watched?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they kind of came together sometimes. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but there were times I was only sleeping a few hours and I had all these big plans for the buildings, like I was going to fix things or organize everything better.\n\nAnd around then, that’s when I’d hear the voice in the vents too… and I felt real sure the hallway lights were sending warnings about me. I know that probably sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so down or hopeless that you lost interest in things you usually care about, or felt that life wasn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, sometimes. I don’t like saying it because I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be a problem or scare anybody.\n\nBut when it gets really dark for me, I’ll catch myself thinking maybe it’d be better if I just didn’t wake up. Or like… if I could just disappear for good, maybe that would be easier. I’m not proud of thinking that. I’m just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood—like feeling really down or really up—happened even when you weren’t having those experiences with the voices or feeling watched?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I think most of the time when my mood gets really off, that other stuff is around too, so it’s hard for me to separate it.\n\nThere might’ve been days I was just down or kind of keyed up without noticing anything else, but I don’t know if I can say for sure. I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was really low or really high, but you weren’t hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I can’t really think of a clean example like that. I don’t want to say no if I’m just not remembering right, but… when my mood gets that far one way or the other, it’s usually all mixed together in my head.\n\nMaybe there were little times, like a bad day or feeling kind of wound up, but not the real strong ones. I don’t know if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was very low or very high, but you were not experiencing any of the other things like hearing voices or feeling like people were watching you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can give you that exact kind of example, doctor. I’m sorry.\n\nWhat I can think of is… there have been some calmer weeks, where I wasn’t really way up or really down. Like I was more level, I guess. But even then, for more than a couple weeks, I was still hearing that man in the vents, and I still felt like people in the building were… sort of signaling about me.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to make it worse than it is. I just… I think that’s what happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when your mood was very low or very high, but you were not experiencing any of the other things like hearing voices or feeling watched at all?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of it the way you’re asking… and honestly, no, not really.\n\nLike, when I had those times where I was only sleeping a few hours and I was making all these big plans for the buildings, I was also hearing that voice from the vents. And I remember feeling pretty sure the hallway lights were sending warnings about me.\n\nSo I don’t think I can say there was a very high time without that other stuff there too… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember a time when your mood was extremely low or extremely high for at least a few days, and during that time you were not experiencing any voices or feelings of being watched at all?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to answer it right, doctor… but I don’t really remember a time like that.\n\nWhen I was sleeping just a few hours and getting all those big ideas about the buildings, that’s when I was also hearing the voice in the vents. And I felt pretty sure the hallway lights were giving warnings about me.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can honestly say there was a few days where my mood was that high or low and none of that was happening. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. There were some calmer weeks, I guess I’d call them that, where I wasn’t especially up or really depressed. I was mostly just getting through things, more normal.

But… the voice from the vents was still there during that time. And I still had that feeling that people in the building were signaling about me. It wasn’t like I was trying to make a big deal out of it, but it did go on for more than a couple weeks, even when my mood wasn’t that extreme.

planning work like sleep is something other people need, not me.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast it feels like a whole crowd talking inside my head, jumping from bills to jail to mosque to cleaning chemicals before I can catch one thought.\n- key: S039\n goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I start calling old coworkers, offering to clean extra buildings, setting up
